Company profile  
Founded in December 1994 and listed in March 1998, Vanguard International Semiconductor Corp. 
(VIS) successfully transformed its business model from memory to a complementary metal-oxide-
semiconductor (CMOS) logic foundry focused on specialty technologies such as mixed-signal, power 
analog and embedded memory, becoming one of the world’s leading specialty foundries. Leveraging its 
strategic relationship with major shareholder TSMC, VIS operates wafer fabs in Taiwan and Singapore, 
serving a wide variety of customers, including fabless chipmakers and IDMs globally.

### Page 5

32

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Fast ramp to capture AI ripple effect 
We initiate coverage of VIS with an Outperform (2) rating and 12M TP of TWD210. 
Founded in December 1994, headquartered in Hsinchu Science Park and listed in March 
1998 (see Appendix 1 for more details on the company), VIS looks set to enjoy fully loaded 
operations (in terms of wafer-in) through 2027, with sustained pricing power, thanks to the 
ripple effect of AI demand scaling from compute to connectivity and power, further 
augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation.

Given its longstanding ties with TSMC, which currently holds a c.19% stake in VIS and has 
strategically helped it advance its business transformation by moving up value verticals of 
specialty-foundry offerings to differentiate itself in the competitive specialty foundry market, 
VIS looks set to benefit from this multi-year AI scaling, among peers. The fast expansion of 
its first 12” fab in Singapore should help cement the AI business and capitalise on what we 
observe as a paradigm shift in the foundry industry, as mentioned in the main section of 
this report.

Fully loaded operations on AI ripple effect 
Like many other semiconductor foundries, VIS enjoyed unusual pricing power during the 
COVID period as the virus, together with geopolitical tensions between China and the US, 
inflated chip demand across virtually all product applications to an unprecedented level. 
Consequently, the industry suffered from an unprecedented 2-year downturn on a 
prolonged inventory correction, if excluding the AI build that has helped rescue the 
advanced foundry segment, save the village (ie, the chip industry) and bring the global chip 
cycle back on its historical track of an average of a one-year downturn. This downturn 
caused choppy operations for many second-tier foundries which focused initially on the 
non-AI space over 2022-25, including VIS (see our series of Technology Outlook reports: 
2023: V-shape, U-shape or double dip?, 2024: The dragon is ready to soar, 2025: likely 
longer and stronger).

Starting from 2H25, after the AI builds scaled out and across from compute into memory to 
connectivity and power, creating a ripple effect in demand that benefits not only advanced 
but also specialty foundries, we expect VIS’s wafer loadings to approach full levels through 
2027 (in terms of wafer-in), despite its fast 12” expansion. With its parent company TSMC’s 
strategic partnership, VIS stands at a strong position to benefit from this multi-year AI 
scaling, in our view. To maximise the AI business potential, VIS is accelerating its first 12” 
fab in Singapore via a JV named VisionPower Semiconductor Manufacturing Corp 
(VSMC), with phase-one investment of USD6.8bn where, in terms of registered capital of 
USD4bn, VIS assumes USD2.4bn for a 60% stake, while NXP assumes USD1.6bn for a 
40% stake.

In terms of the total investment on a cash basis, VIS will chip in only USD1.0bn, with 
USD2.8bn coming from NXP and USD1.1bn from TSMC with some equipment 
consignment, while the rest will come from other customer prepayments, loans and a 
subsidy from the Singapore government. Upon VSMC’s completion, this will bring VIS a 
total of 6 fabs, with combined capacity reaching up to c.380,000 8”-equivalent wafers per 
month (wpm) by end-2028, based on our forecasts (see table on next page for details of 
our key business assumptions).

Fast expansion to capture the paradigm shift 
On top of the AI scaling creating a ripple effect for demand spillover, such strength looks to 
be further augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation, as assessed in the main section of this 
report. To capitalise on the likely paradigm shift in the specialty foundry segment by 
TSMC’s fab optimisation, VIS is pulling in its first 12” fab construction around half a year 
ahead of schedule, aiming to start mass production from 1Q27 after it hosted an opening 
ceremony on 28 September.

Registered under VSMC, we expect this fab to reach its first-phase designed capacity of 
44,000 wpm by end-2028, subject to market dynamics. c.30% of the fab’s capacity will be 
dedicated to TSMC’s wafer production of silicon interposers handling chip-on-wafer-on-
substrate (CoWoS) advanced packaging, which has seen meaningful supply constraint 
due to strong AI high-performance compute (HPC) demand. The rest of the capacity is for 
other customers’ power semiconductors (such as mixed-signal, analog and PMIC) and 
embedded flash, focusing on AI scaling and automotive and industrial (A&I) applications.

Despite the 12” expansion pull-in, we do not expect the ramp to dwarf capacity utilisations 
since all the planned capacity is already sold out, thanks to the long-term agreements 
(LTA) signed with customers amid the supply constraints, which should help VIS manage 
surging depreciation and minimise margin dilution for financial resilience. Although this 12” 
fab has little to do with TSMC’s 28/22/20nm fab optimisation addressed in the main 
section, since it covers only process nodes ranging from 0.13u to 40nm and thus enjoys 
only the 65/55nm wafer interposer spillover from TSMC, on our market research, we see 
possibilities for TSMC to shut down more legacy 12” fabs in the future. We believe this may 
incentivise VIS to speed up its second 12” fab construction in Singapore, namely the 
phase-two, or VSMC Fab 2, for 40,000-50,000 wpm capacity beyond 2028 and is scalable 
to 28nm-and-below and even the finFET (FF) process nodes.

Financial forecasts 
In the near term, we expect VIS to enjoy counter-seasonal quarters through 2027, thanks 
to the ripple effect of AI scaling on wafer loadings and pricing power. We estimate that VIS 
earned 3Q26 EPS of TWD1.57, in line with the Bloomberg consensus estimate. For 4Q26, 
we are behind consensus since 1) we expect a diminished effect of price hikes after our 
estimated 2-3% QoQ EPS increases in 3Q26; and 2) we do not expect the 12” contribution 
to ramp that soon. In the long term, we forecast VIS to deliver a revenue CAGR of nearly 
30% over 2026-28 for an EPS CAGR of 26%.

Revenue and customers 
As of 2Q26, power management IC (PMIC) accounted for the majority of revenue (77%; 
see chart on next page), on a rising trend from 40% a decade ago thanks to the co-efforts 
between VIS and TSMC strategically transforming the former’s business up the value 
verticals from logic commodity display drivers (D/D) for consumer electronics devices to 
power discrete, analog, and PMIC for not only consumer but A&I applications. Riding on 
the escalating trend of demand for advanced high-V PMIC accommodating AI servers and 
power infrastructure under the AI cycle, we expect the PMIC revenue contribution to rise 
further over our forecast period, while the D/D contribution will drop to c.10%-plus for base-
line customer demand support.

As of 2Q26, VIS found 16%, 16%, and 46% of revenue from major process nodes of 
0.25u, 0.18u, and 0.13u-and-below, respectively, on our estimates. Since the VSMC fab 
will focus on 0.13u all the way to 40nm, and TSMC’s wafer interposers will take c.30% of 
the capacity ramp on our estimates focusing on 65/55nm, we expect the 0.13u-and-below 
to increase to more than 60% of revenue over our forecast period. Both the PMIC and 
node advances should be blended ASP and margin accretive, helping mitigate the 
depreciation burden associated with the VSMC fab ramp. Illustrated in a chart on the next 
page, we expect VIS’s blended ASP to trend upward from c.USD630 in 2Q26, rising by 1-
5% QoQ through our forecast period to exceed USD700 by 2028.

VIS services a high variety of customers around the world, including most fabless 
chipmakers in Taiwan and mainland China focusing on the D/D, power MOSFET and 
PMIC, and most integrated device manufacturers (IDM) across Japan, Europe and the US 
focusing on power semiconductor foundry works.

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Cost analysis 
Over the past decades, VIS’s cost structure has fluctuated in fixed overheads (mainly D&A) 
from time to time, depending on its fab acquisitions, varying from 10% to 30% of COGS 
(refer back to the key assumption table). Given VSMC is about to ramp in production with a 
total capex of USD6.8bn for phase one, or roughly 2x VIS’s 2025-26E aggregate revenue, 
we expect the D&A portion to return to the 30% level over our forecast period, acting as a 
margin dilutor. Yet with VIS’s capex budget of TWD60-70bn (c.USD2bn) this year, we 
expect the capex to largely subside from 2027 for the depreciation cycle to peak in 2029. 
As a result, we forecast its D&A in absolute terms to rise by 54% YoY in 2027 to TWD14bn 
and 33% YoY in 2028 to nearly TWD19bn, with gross margin (GM) stalling at 32-34% over 
2Q26-1Q28, then picking up to 35% or higher in 2H28 and likely rising further after the 
depreciation comes off the cycle into 2029.

Minority interest 
Minority interest (MI), or non-controlling interest as stated by VIS, kicked in from 1Q25 and 
rose to 2.4% of pretax income in 2Q26 due to preparation for the VSMC investment. We 
forecast the MI to rise to TWD341m this year (TWD137m in 2025), TWD568m in 2027 and 
TWD1.45bn in 2028, mainly from the 40% MI owned by NXP as VIS ramps the 12” fab 
investment in Singapore. As illustrated in a table below, we forecast VSMC to record 
revenue of nearly TWD9bn in 2027 and TWD26bn in 2028 for operating loss of TWD1.4bn 
and operating profit of TWD3.6bn, respectively, on the basis of a standalone operating 
entity.

Dividend policy 
Over the past decade, VIS has stepped up its dividend payout ratios from 50% in 2011 to 
more than 100% this year. We expect the company to keep the ratios at c.80% over our 
forecast period, subject to the AI development which should determine the capex 
requirement for the VSMC investment, affecting its FCF generating power. VIS paid a 
TWD4.5/share cash dividend this year (against last year’s earnings), and we forecast it to 
pay TWD5.0/share for 2027 with a payout ratio of 85% against this year’s earnings for a 
2.7% yield based on the closing price as of 5 October.

Cashflow and gearing 
Given its hefty capex over 2025-27E, we expect VIS’s free cashflow (FCF) to turn negative 
during the same period. Foreseeing the cash gap, VIS issued new shares of 228m in 2024 
for a 12% per-share dilution and collected TWD17.6bn. It further issued ECBs in February 
this year to collect nearly TWD11bn; therefore, we forecast its debt-to-equity (D/E) ratio to 
approach 170% in 2026. Yet with the VSMC ramp in profitability for operating cash inflow, 
we forecast the FCF to turn positive in 2028, helping lower the gearing to c.140%.

Valuation and risk 
We employ an ROE-adjusted PBR methodology to value the foundry stocks under our 
coverage, where we compare a foundry’s projected ROE with its historical ROE average, 
work out a premium or discount, and apply it to the foundry’s historical average PBR for 
our ROE-adjusted PBR target. Given TSMC is VIS’s strategic partner, we tweak our 
methodology a bit by anchoring VIS’s ROE-adjusted PBR to TSMC’s, where we compare 
their long-term ROE projections (2004-28E), work out a discount of 33% and then apply 
the discount to our 7.7x target PBR for TSMC, resulting in our target PBR of 5.2x and 12M 
TP of TWD210 for VIS on our one-year forward BVPS forecast.

Key downside risks to our call would be: 1) the current chip upcycle ends earlier than 
expected; and 2) VSMC’s 12” fab ramp/execution comes in below expectation.

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Appendix: company profile 
Founded in December 1994 with headquarters located in Hsinchu Science Park and 
shares listed in March 1998, Vanguard International Semiconductor (VIS) is a leading 
specialty foundry in the world focusing on mix signal, power semiconductor and embedded 
memory chip manufacturing for value-added differentiation against competition in the 
mature foundry market. With strategic help from its parent company TSMC on business 
transformation and technology transfer, VIS commanded a 1.1% share in the global 
foundry market by 2025 revenue on our estimates (total size c.USD140bn), or nearly 4% in 
the global mature foundry market (ex-TSMC as a proxy), behind SMIC, UMC, 
GlobalFoundries (GF) and Huahong Grace Semiconductor (HHGrace; see chart on the 
next page). Note: our foundry market calculation is for dedicated foundry works only, 
different from TSMC’s revised definition of the Foundry 2.0 that includes not only the 
dedicated foundry but packaging, testing and other non-memory semiconductor 
manufacturing services.

The foundry market 
Ever since TSMC’s founder innovated the semiconductor foundry service model, the 
foundry market has been in operation for more than 3 decades. Thanks to a rise in the 
global AI buildout, the global foundry revenue, after a dip in 2023 on the post-COVID 
inventory correction, recovered by more than 20% YoY pa over 2024-25. We forecast the 
growth to accelerate to more than 30% YoY this year, with revenue reaching USD186bn 
and exceeding USD255bn in 2028 for a robust 23% CAGR over 2025-28. If excluding 
TSMC, we forecast the market to grow by 14% YoY this year and approach USD60bn in 
2028 for a CAGR of 13%, where we expect VIS to outgrow the market growth over our 
forecast period as illustrated in a chart below, thanks to its fast capacity expansion to 
capitalise on the AI scaling and paradigm shift in the specialty foundry segment.

Operation and customer 
VIS currently operates 5 wafer fabs with 4 in Taiwan, including Fab 1 which commenced 
operations in 1994 as a memory setup and then converted to mixed signal, power metal 
oxide semiconductor field effect transistor (MOSFET) and logic foundry fab; Fab 2 which 
was acquired in 2007 from Winbond; Fab 3 which was acquired from Nanya Tech in 2014; 
and Fab 5 which was acquired from AUO in 2021, plus 1 in Singapore, Fab VS1, acquired 
from GF in 2019 and previously named 3E. The 5 fabs currently operate a total of 280,000 
wpm capacity all in 8” wafer size, in which the Fab VS1 and 5 are scalable for an extra 
10,000 wpm capacity each, though VIS doesn’t seem to be keen in exercising the 
scalability since VSMC apparently is the focus for expansion. Following its opening 
ceremony on 28 September this year, VIS’s first 12” fab via VSMC is slated for mass-
production from 1Q27, aimed to bring up total capacity to c.380,000 wpm in 8” equivalent 
wafer by end-2028.

VIS doesn’t disclose its key customers due to confidentiality issues, but we believe its key 
customers cover most of the fabless chipmakers in Taiwan and mainland China focusing 
on display driver (D/D), power MOSFET and power management IC (PMIC), as well as 
integrated device manufacturers (IDM) across Japan, Europe and the US. According to our 
industry survey, key D/D vendors in the greater China region are Novatek (3034 TT, 
TWD529, Hold [3]), Himax (HIMX US, not rated), Raydium (3592 TT, not rated), Ilitek (6962 
TT, not rated), Sitronix Technology (8016 TT, not rated), FocalTech (3545 TT, not rated),

Technology (GMT; 8081 TT, not rated) and Niko Semiconductor (3317 TT, not rated).

Shareholder structure 
VIS’s major shareholders were TSMC and the Taiwan government’s Development Funds, 
with c.19% and c.16% of holdings, respectively, as of August 2026 under total share 
outstanding of 1.88bn. With management in aggregate holding an insignificant stake and 
the rest being held by institutional investors (QFII holding was 32.7% as of 5 October), we 
estimate a healthy free float of more than 60% for the stock, with decent cap of c.USD11bn 
and 3-month daily turnover of more than USD100m.

TSMC reduced its holding in VIS this year by 8pp, raising investor concerns on whether 
the move would jeopardise the former’s strategic support to the latter. We see no direct link 
between the stake reduction and strategic support, and believe TSMC will continue the 
support, evidenced by its licence of gallium-nitride (GaN) and interposer technologies and 
equipment consignment to VSMC.

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
VIS's board of directors (BoD) consists of 9 members, including 5 independent directors,
representing 56% of the total board seats. The board comprises members with diverse
backgrounds in business management, finance, industry, and academia, providing a broad
range of expertise and perspectives.
We believe the majority-independent board structure and the directors’ collective experience in
leadership, corporate governance, industry expertise, financial management, risk management,
and sustainability strengthen the effectiveness of the BoD and support VIS's long-term growth
and corporate governance practices.
Capital management 1 VIS pays its dividend on a quarterly basis. In the past 5 years, it has managed its dividendpayout well, as evidenced by its dividend in dollar terms, where it has never lowered its dividend.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were around 1-2% of its total revenue each year. The percentage isinsignificant and thus we see limited risk for VIS.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
VIS has established a comprehensive sustainable supply chain management framework to
ensure suppliers comply with its sustainability requirements covering environmental protection,
labor and human rights, occupational health and safety, business ethics, and responsible
sourcing. The company requires suppliers to adhere to its "VIS Supplier Code of Conduct" and
"Corporate Sustainability Policy", and conducts supplier sustainability assessments and audits
as part of its supplier management process. We believe these efforts strengthen VIS's supply
chain resilience and help mitigate supply chain risks.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
VIS is committed to improving water efficiency and sustainable water management. In 2022, the
company implemented the ISO 46001 Water Efficiency Management System to analyze water
consumption patterns, identify significant water-use equipment, evaluate water-saving
opportunities, and establish improvement measures. VIS also provides annual water
management training at its Taiwan facilities to promote continuous improvement in water-use
efficiency. We believe these efforts demonstrate the company's commitment to responsible
water stewardship and help reduce operational risks associated with water resources.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
VIS adopts a comprehensive resource management approach to waste management and has
established waste management procedures in accordance with ISO 14001 standards. Through
dedicated waste management personnel and regular employee training, the company promotes
effective waste reduction, classification, collection, storage, and treatment practices across its
operations.
E GHG emissions 1
VIS adopts a source-segregation and multi-stage treatment approach, supported by high-
efficiency air pollution control systems, to effectively manage VOC and acidic/alkaline gas
emissions from semiconductor manufacturing processes.
The company's emission levels remain well below regulatory limits, reflecting its commitment to
environmental compliance and sustainable operations.

### Page 1

Summary: Nanya Technology (Nanya) announced its 3Q26 results after market hours on 
12 October. Its revenue of TWD133.6m (+62% QoQ, +612% YoY) and operating profit of 
TWD106.9m (+76% QoQ, +95.5x YoY) were robust. While shipments decreased by a mid-
single-digit % QoQ, ASP increased by > 60% QoQ mainly due to renewed LTA contracts. 
While DDR4 accounted for >60% of revenue, management expects the favourable pricing 
environment to persist on robust demand and supply constraints. However, due to massive 
capex (c.USD16bn) on the build-out of a new 50k wpm capacity, management didn’t 
announce an enhanced shareholder return plan or a higher dividend payout.

Key highlights

• 3Q26 results review. Nanya's 3Q26 revenue of TWD133.6m (+62% QoQ, +612% YoY) 
and operating profit of TWD106.9m (+76% QoQ, +95.5x YoY) were slightly below our 
and recent market estimates due to higher employee compensation expenses. Its 
DRAM ASP increased by >60% QoQ in 3Q26 on LTA price adjustments, while 
shipments decreased by a mid-single-digit % QoQ. DDR5 accounted for >10% of 3Q26 
revenue, while DDR4 accounted for >60%. Management guided for a persistent market 
shortage and a continued price uptrend in 4Q26, while shipments are expected to 
remain flat until wafer output from the new fab comes online in 4Q27.

• Key takeaways from conference call. Management expects AI infrastructure to keep 
driving DRAM demand. While AI infrastructure and server applications currently account 
for >20% of revenue, it expects this exposure to grow to >30% through 2029-30. In 
particular, DDR4 demand now accounts for 12-14% of total DRAM market demand, 
maintaining the higher ASP trend on strong demand. With major DRAM players having 
almost completed their HBM supply negotiations for 2027, management believes it 
would be hard to increase supply given capacity allocation and longer lead times. Its 
LTA output portion was >20%, and management expects this to increase going forward. 
The company announced a new fab construction plan for 30k wpm by the end of 2028 
and up to 50k wpm by 2030 to respond to LTA demand from AI infrastructure. Despite 
the solid 3Q26 earnings, there was no announcement of an increased dividend payout, 
due to the capex burden (c.USD16bn for 50k wpm). It did not guide for any share 
buyback.

Recommendation 
We have a Buy (1) call on Nanya with a 12M TP of TWD700, based on a 7.4x PER on the 
2026-27E EPS. While the robust 3Q26 earnings led by the significant ASP hike was 
positive, we view the delay in announcing a shareholder return plan as disappointing. Key 
risk: weaker-than-expected commodity DRAM demand due to delays in data centre build-
outs.

Takeaways on NPO from 2026

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
On Day 2 of the 2026 OCP Global Summit, an IPEC optical interconnect workshop

focused on discussions around NPO. Industry participants such as Source Photonics,
Lightcounting, Alibaba, Meta, Oracle, Microsoft, Ciena, Ligent, Genuine Optics,
Lumentum, Lumilens, NVIDIA and Huawei presented their views on NPO.
The industry has formed the view connectivity, rather than compute alone, is
becoming the primary bottleneck. Near-packaged optics (NPO) move optical
conversion closer to compute, targeting better signal integrity, bandwidth, and

energy efficiency. OpenCPX explicitly targets common mechanical, electrical,
optical, thermal, and management interfaces across suppliers, providing the
ecosystem with a shared implementation framework rather than only a broad
architectural vision.
However, we also observed debate over whether pluggable NPO offers materially
better flexibility and serviceability than CPO. Key concern on CPO is that optical
failure could result in scrapping an entire ASIC assembly.

Overall, we observed growing discussion and interests in NPO, implying a greater
likelihood of accelerated commercialization over the next 12-18 months, in our view.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Connectivity has become the bottleneck, and NPO is a potential solution. We
observed growing discussion and interest in NPO at the conference.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

動態更新 IC製造 ‧ 台灣

台積電 
(2330.TW /2330 TT)

多、更多、再更多的 N3 產能擴張 
重要訊息 
台積電 3Q26 財報表現符合預期，管理層預估 2026 年營收將年增 44%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 符合預期，4Q26 展望強勁。3Q26 毛利率 66.4%，受 N2 量產爬坡稀
釋影響，較我們預估低 1.6 個百分點，但符合公司財測。受惠營收成長及營
業費用控管，營業利益及營益率均符合預期。台積電 4Q26 營收財測為 523
億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增 14%，較我們先前預估高出 7%，毛
利率及營益率則預期持平，主要受惠 HPC 需求強勁及 N2 量產爬坡。 
N3 持續擴產，並評估美國亞利桑那州以外的擴產機會 。隨 4Q26 展望轉
強，台積電將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先 40%以上上調至 44%，並
維持全年資本支出 600 億至 640 億美元。台積電過去鮮少將單一製程產能
擴充至超過原先規劃的目標( 約每月 15 萬片)。然而，除近期宣布的三座晶圓
廠(台南 Fab 18 、亞利桑那州二廠及熊本二廠) 外，台積電再宣布於台南新增
一座 N3 晶圓廠，預計於 1H28 量產。整體而言，我們預估 N3 產能將由
2026 年底的每月 15 萬至 16 萬片，在未來兩年進一步增加 8 萬片，增幅達
50%。我們認為此次擴產反映三項需求：(1)Agentic AI 帶動的 CPU 需求；
(2)HBM4e 客製化基底晶粒；以及(3)AI ASIC 。此外，台積電亦正評估在美國
亞利桑那州以外地區擴產的可能性，例如德州，但目前尚未做出最終決定。  
不會將任何商機拱手讓給競爭對手 。管理層多次強調不會讓競爭對手取得任
何商機，體現在 N3 產能的積極擴充。此外，儘管我們預期 Google(美 )委託
Intel(美 )生產的 Humufish 目前沒有備援計劃。然而，在必要情況下，台積電
將協助客戶確保產品如期交付， 暗示若 Intel 的 EMIB-T 製程進度落後，台積
電有望進一步提升市占率。部分投資人可能擔憂台積電導入 ASML High -NA
設備的時程較競爭對手晚兩年，進而影響其技術領先地位。但我們持不同看
法，採用最新設備並不必然代表技術領先。若 在 2030 年前台積電能在不投
資昂貴設備的情況下達到相近的製程效能，反而 有望取得相較同業更顯著的
成本優勢。 
投資建議  
儘管 3Q26 財報符合預期，且我們預估 4Q26 表現將更為強勁，但在市場預
期極高下，投資人仍可能對短期毛利率走勢有所疑慮。考量未來的成長動能
及穩健的執行能力，我們建議投資人趁股價回檔時逢低布局。我們僅微幅調
整獲利預估， 但隨評價基準遞延至 2028 年，我們 將目標價上調至 3,650
元，仍基於景氣上行週期平均的 20 倍本益比，維持「增加持股」評等 。 
投資風險  
全球景氣不確定性；積極擴產導致供給過剩。

法說會重點數據 
 4Q26 財測： 營收預估為 523 億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增
14%；毛利率 65-67%，營益率 56.5- 58.5%。 
 2026 年展望：全年營收成長預估上調至年增 44%，維持資本支出 600
億至 640 億美元。  
 毛利率稀釋：海外擴產預計稀釋 2026 年毛利率 2 個百分點，未來幾年
稀釋幅度將擴大至 3-4 個百分點。  
 產能規劃：  
 N2 製程家族產能預計於 2027 年增加至兩倍以上。  
 台南新增 N3 晶圓廠預計於 1H27 量產，另一座 N3 晶圓廠則規劃於
1H28 量產。 
 亞利桑那州 N3 二廠已完成廠房建設，目前正進行設備搬入及安
裝，預計於 3Q27 量產，略早於原訂時程。  
 日本 N3 製程預計於 2028 年量產。 
 A14 製程已開始進行產能準備，預計於 2027 年如期試產，並於
2028 年量產。

ESG 圖表定義  
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1天 = 8小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，凱基

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
TSMC was founded in 1987 and is listed in Taiwan 
(2330TT) and the US (TSM/NYS).TSMC is the largest and 
global leader in integrated circuit (IC) manufacturing. As a 
build-to-order foundry, it provides a wide range of value-
add activities: IC manufacturing, mask-making, IC design 
services, turnkey solutions, and process development. We 
attribute its success to its proven, winning business model, 
unparalleled scale advantage, optimized execution, and 
technology scope and depth. 5 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
The ongoing semi content growth in mobile, rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI), and proliferation of Internet of Things (IoT) 
should result in sustainable upside in aggregate computing 
power globally. TSMC, as the leading contract manufacturer 
of semiconductor chips, is in a good position to capitalize. 
We are structurally positive on TSMC and expect it to 
sustain over 15% growth with rising FCF from 2026 
onwards.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMWF / TSM) 
Our PO for TSMC is NT$3,260 per share (US$620 per ADR), based on 20x 2027E P/E. 
TSMC's valuation has traded in the range of 10-26x PE from 2017. 20x is in the mid to 
high end of the historical range, above the average P/E of 16x. We think a PE above the 
average is justified given TSMC's enhanced industry position and mid-to-high 50% 5-
year CAGR in AI should underpin a rerating

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) greater-than-expected slowdown in global 
smartphone/consumer electronics demand, (2) Intel's potential insourcing strategy and 
ambitions in foundry service, (3) execution risks on advanced nodes, and (4) higher than 
expected tariffs.

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원 
현재주가 (10.6) 272,000원 
상승여력 83.8%

The Japan Research Institute, Limited

Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

### Page 3

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

4 
−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private -sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and 
intangible social infrastructure that promotes investment has been established. 
Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
VN-Index có thể hồi kỹ thuật từ cạnh dưới tam giác, song việc chớm thủng hỗ trợ 
1.710 điểm và số mã còn giữ xu hướng tăng từ đáy tháng 7 suy gi ảm khiến xu 
hướng ngắn hạn trở nên trung tính.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Document 316: Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas

### Page 1

September 2026: Marked improvement in
services business confidence
September 2026: Business climate in the
manufacturing sector comfortably in the
expansion zone for the 8th month in a row

to a 3-year high
Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas

Document 321: Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07)

### Page 1

7 October 2026

FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.

Document 322: First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#consumer #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ household incomes, assets and savings
update - June quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
10:24 October 08 2026 Share
New Zealand incomes and savings are rising, as is the value of households’ financial
assets. However, the value of housing assets remains flat.
Key takeouts from Stats NZ’s latest update on household incomes, assets and savings (June quarter
2026)

### Page 2

Household incomes
Disposable income levels in the economy grew by 5.2% in the year to June, down from 6.5% in the
year to March

Savings and wealth
Household savings have continued to rise, climbing by $2.5b over the past quarter.

Overall household wealth levels were up 1.9% over the past year. Under the surface, the make-up of
household wealth continues to shift.

Interest costs 
Overall household income levels are continuing to rise. Much of that is due to increases in
entrepreneurial earnings (which is in part due to increased earnings in the agricultural sector). Wage
and salary growth has been more limited but has picked up.
Household savings levels have been rising for three years now, with another solid rise over the past
three months. 
Overall household wealth levels have risen modestly. Although the value of households’ financial
assets is continuing to rise, housing assets have essentially shown no growth since late 2023.
Household spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter. That’s the first increase in
two years. The easing in average borrowing costs seen over the past few years has come to a close
with the RBNZ’s hiking cycle now in train.
Underpinning the rise in overall income levels have been continued increases in entrepreneurial
earnings, which are up 12% over the past year. That will include stronger earnings in the agricultural
sector, which has been boosted by the strength in commodity export prices. That lift in agricultural
earnings has been reflected in stronger economic confidence and spending in many rural regions.
We expect commodity export earnings will remain firm over the year ahead. 
In contrast, wage and salary earnings are up just 2.7% over the past year. That’s up from 1.8% in the
year to March, but it’s a fairly muted rise, especially given the continued increases in the population
and inflation of 4.1%.
June’s large increase in savings builds on the solid increases seen over the past three years.
The savings rate in the June quarter was 3.7% of disposable incomes, down just slightly from last
quarter.
The value of households’ financial assets has continued to rise and is up 4.6% over the past year. 
However, the value of housing and land assets is down 0.9% over the past year, and it has remained
essentially unchanged since late 2023. We expect housing market conditions will remain soft over
the coming year, limiting increases in overall household wealth. 
Households’ spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter, climbing by 0.3%.
While that’s a relatively small change, it is the first increase that we’ve seen in two years.

### Page 3

Related articles

Media Contact:
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist
P: +64 9 336 5668
M: +64 21 710 852
Over the past few years, many borrowers saw their debt servicing costs fall as they rolled off earlier
high mortgage rates and onto lower ones. That helped to support household spending.
However, the easing in average borrowing cost has now ended. Recent months have seen mortgage
rates pushing higher as the RBNZ has begun to hike the Official Cash Rate, with further hikes
expected over the coming year. 
Looking ahead, we don’t expect a significant increase in average household borrowing costs.
However, we won’t see the same support for spending that we did over the past few years.

Document 365: Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

23 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
NEXT GENERATION EU: THE PROGRAMME IS COMING TO AN END, BUT ITS EFFECTS ON ECONOMIC ACTIVITY WILL
ENDURE
Guillaume Derrien
A pillar of the NextGenerationEU programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) will expire on December 31, 2026. As of the
end of August 2026, slightly more than three-quarters of the total budget had been disbursed (EUR 440 billion out of EUR 573 billion).
Disbursing the remaining quarter (EUR 133 billion) by the end of the year will require a significant increase compared with the histo-

rical pace (EUR 90 billion per year). The goal of channeling these funds toward productive investment appears to have been achieved.
Tangible results for investment in Europe
According to Eurostat, public spending co-financed by the RRF totaled EUR 310 billion between 2020 and 2025. Nearly three-quarters (74%)
consisted of capital expenditures, including EUR 106 billion in direct public investment and EUR 122 billion in capital transfers to the private

sector (such as grants). This spending supported investment by businesses and households.
The list of major programmes funded by the RFF, published by the European Commission 1, shows that, within this scope, nearly two-thirds of
the funding went to transportation (primarily rail), energy infrastructure, and digital infrastructure (see Chart 1).
Another notable point: capital expenditures co-financed by the RRF increased significantly, reaching 0.4% of GDP for the European Union as a

whole in 2025 (see Chart 2). There are significant disparities among Member States, primarily due to differences in the amounts allocated to
them. Croatia (1.9%), Greece (1.2%), Italy (1%), and Spain (0.7%) received significant support, in contrast to France and Germany (0.1%). These
figures are consistent with the IMF’s estimate that the RRF added approximately 0.3 percentage points (pp) to EU growth in 2025 (more than
1 pp in Greece and Croatia, around 0.8 pp in Italy, and 0.5 pp in Spain) 2. Furthermore, as the first major joint European borrowing initiative, the

RRF made it possible to finance investment without increasing national budget deficits, giving several countries (Spain, Italy, Greece, Portugal)
valuable breathing room to consolidate their public finances.

### Page 2

Chart of the Week / 23 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
What impact on EU’s economic activity can we expect once this programme ends?
At first glance, effects will be limited. The end of the programme is expected to weigh on both public and private investment. However, the
strengthening of economic activity that we anticipate (see details in our latest Ecoperspectives – Q3 2026) rests primarily on German public

investment programmes and structural drivers (investment in AI, defence spending).
The ECB makes the same point in its September macroeconomic projections 3. It emphasises that the end of the RRF is expected to weigh
on public investment in 2027, while it anticipates an acceleration in business investment. Furthermore, RRF funds are being absorbed on
the ground only gradually. Since most disbursements are concentrated toward the end of the programme, the effects of the RRF will extend

beyond 2026.
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Stopping for gas
• We estimate that New Zealand consumer
prices rose by 0.7% in the September quarter,
with a moderation in fuel costs balanced
against increases in council rates, building
costs and food prices.
• The annual inflation rate is expected to drop
back to 3.7% (down from 4.1% in the year
to  June).
• Measures of core inflation are expected to
remain firm, with some closely watched
measures expected to rise to the top of the

RBNZ’s target band.
• Our forecast is a little below the RBNZ’s last
published forecast for 3.9% annual inflation.

We expect the September quarter inflation report (out
on 22 October) will show that New Zealand consumer
prices rose 0.7% over the past three months. That would
see the annual inflation rate slipping back to 3.7%, down
from 4.1% in the year to June. However, as we discuss

in our recent forecast update, that’s likely to be just a
temporary reprieve with the recent rise in oil prices set to
push inflation up again over the coming months.
16 Oct 2026 | Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
CPI preview, September quarter 2026 –
Thursday 22 October, 10:45am.

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Key drivers of quarterly inflation.
Underpinning our forecast for 0.7% inflation in the
September quarter are some big price movements in
both directions.
The largest move in prices this quarter has actually been
a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by any
stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for
both petrol and diesel over the September quarter were
lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed

higher in the wake of the Middle East conflict – see the
chart below. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91
unleaded was around 6% lower in September than in the
June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower.
Fuel spending accounts for around 4% of the CPI, and
these moves have taken 0.4ppts off inflation this quarter.
New Zealand petrol prices – 91 unleaded

While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in
the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully high. And
that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other
goods and services. Of note, the September quarter
saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with
international airfares up 14% and domestic airfares up

nearly 8%. Together those prices account for around 2%
of the CPI.
We also expect building costs (10% of the CPI) will rise by
1.6% this quarter following a similarly chunky rise in June.
Again, much of that rise has been related to higher global
oil prices which have been flowing through to higher
operating and material costs. That’s occurred at the same
time as the amount of planned construction work has
been rising.
The September quarter also sees the annual increases

in local council rates and charges for services like water
supply (around 4% of the CPI). We expect these costs will
be up around 7.4% this year, continuing the run of large
increases seen in previous years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices
(18% of the CPI), largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
the price for fresh vegetables.
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in
housing rents, which are the largest component of

the CPI, accounting for 10% of the basket. With low
population growth and ample supply, rents have
effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual
terms, rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more
than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Source: Westpac
Where could we be surprised?
There are some key areas where we see the risk
of a downside surprise. The first is the price for
communications equipment, like mobile phones. After
a large rise in the June quarter, we could see a sizeable

drop in prices this quarter.
There could also be softness in used car prices, especially
with high fuel prices encouraging a shift away from
combustion engine vehicles.
However, the risks aren’t all to one side. Demand for more
environmentally friendly new cars has picked up, and that
could push up prices for those vehicles. In addition, we’re
watching to see if high fuel costs are spilling over into
other prices as we’re seeing in sectors like construction.

Core inflation around 3%, not easing.
With big swings in fuel costs and concerns that this is
spilling over into a broader lift in inflation pressures,
the RBNZ will be paying close attention to the various
measures of core inflation (core inflation measures
smooth through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual
prices and instead track the underlying trend in inflation).
We expect that core inflation measures will point to
continued firmness in underlying price pressures, with

several closely watched measures likely to take a step
higher this quarter. In terms of specifics, we expect:
• CPI ex-fuel inflation: +3.0% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel and food: +3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs
+2.5% previously)

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
We’ll also be watching the distribution of price
changes to see if there’s been a material increase in
the number of prices that are increasing. That could
be an early indication that businesses, even those
with a limited exposure to fuel costs, are starting to
factor in expectations of continued high inflation when
setting prices.
As we’ve noted before, the various measures of core
inflation were not low even before the recent fuel shock.

That means the RBNZ has limited headroom to absorb
upside inflation surprises.
Much of that firmness in core inflation has been due
to increases in domestically oriented non-tradables
prices. We estimate these prices rose by 1.3% over the
September quarter. That would see annual non-tradables
inflation rising to 3.6%, up from 3.4% last quarter.
Non-tradables inflation is a key focus for the RBNZ and
it’s been running at these above-average levels for around

a year now. That’s despite the softness in economic
growth and the labour market. In part, this firmness in
domestic inflation is due to continued large increases in
administered prices, like council rates.
On the imported front, lower fuel costs are expected to
see tradables prices falling by 0.2% over the quarter.
That would result in annual tradables inflation dropping
back to 3.9% from 4.9% previously. Excluding food and
fuel costs, we estimate that tradables prices were up

2.2% over the past year (vs 1.9% in the year to June). That
would be the highest level in three years with the fall in
the NZD boosting import costs.

Westpac
forecasts
How do our forecasts compare to the
RBNZ’s assumptions?
The RBNZ’s last published forecast from their September

policy statement assumed that consumer prices would
rise by 0.8%qtr (3.9%yr). That’s slightly higher than we’re
expecting. Most of the difference is because the RBNZ is
expecting higher imported inflation.
A result in line with our forecast would give the RBNZ a
little more breathing room ahead of their upcoming policy
review on 28 October. We’re expecting they will leave the
OCR on hold at this meeting, while markets are pricing in
a roughly 66% chance of a 25bp hike.

However, even if the overall inflation result is close to
forecast, the underlying detail will be important. The
RBNZ will be watching closely for signs that high fuel
prices are spilling over into other prices, especially with
global oil prices taking another step higher recently. With
that in mind, the strength of the various core inflation
measures will be a key focus for the RBNZ.
Furthermore, while annual inflation is expected to
soften in the September quarter, this is likely to be just a

temporary reprieve. With a sharp rise in fuel costs over
the past few weeks, inflation is set to reaccelerate back
over 4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain
above the RBNZ’s forecast for most of next year.
September monthly prices.
Our forecast for a 0.7% rise in consumer prices in the
September quarter incorporates information from
Stats NZ’s latest monthly prices update. That update
did reveal some softer than expected food price

movements in September. However, that was balanced
against larger than expected rises in airfares and holiday
accommodation costs.

受美伊互相攻擊油輪 、胡塞組織控制曼德海峽及沙

20.1%。上半年在美伊衝突下帶動國防類股大漲 ，
帶動工業產業預估本益比達到近30年的高點27倍。
雖然近期工業受到高油價與國防政策利空已經回檔
7%，但9/22預估本益比仍高達 22.6倍，高於過去
五年平均20.9倍，也高於整體標普500的19.1倍。

重點摘要

壓力
《油價與運價維持高檔，航空產業需求遭遇逆風》
近期受到中東衝突升溫導致僅有少量油輪通過荷姆
茲海峽，貨櫃船實際上仍無法通行。過去5-6年全球
港口吞吐運量成長遠低於船舶訂單量成長 ，導致重
大港口的貨櫃塞港嚴重 。中東地緣政治風險和全球
性的港口擁擠預估短期內都未能改善契機 ，因此未
來半年貨櫃運價仍將維持高位 。由於過去半年美國
機票價格已大幅上漲 ，導致價格敏感的美國旅客消
費開始偏向保守。美國運輸安全管理局(簡稱TSA)統
計每日旅客吞吐量近期呈現持續下滑 ，顯示航空業
持續性漲價已經衝擊到旅客需求 。隨著機構上調

4Q26原油預測 10%到88美元，高價位的航空燃油
價格也將再次對全球航空公司運量與獲利造成衝擊 。
《眾議院通過限制擴大對伊朗軍事行動 ，對國防類
股形成壓力》
今年上半年因中東衝突升溫及美伊緊張情勢 ，國防
股曾出現一波強勁上漲 。但隨著美國眾議院 9/15以
220票對204票通過限制總統對伊朗軍事行動的決議
案，要求未獲國會授權前不得持續或擴大對伊朗的
軍事行動 。雖然美國國會決議案主要是限制新增的
軍事行動授權 ，並非削減國防預算 。但美國國會限
制進一步軍事行動 ，已經造成市場資金對國防類股
進行獲利了結 。考量航空類股仍將面臨高油價逆風 ，
以及美國眾議院擴大限制政府對伊朗軍事行動 。本
行對工業產業展望維持中立評等。
資料來源：TSA、JP Morgan，2026/9/17，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/24

Document 551: 中国・７-９月 GDP は前年比＋4.5％に加速も、外需依存の構図は変わらず ～供給サイドが成長をけん引する一方、個人消費と不動産は_f9a4f0d8

Source PDF: all report pdf/中国・７-９月 GDP は前年比＋4.5％に加速も、外需依存の構図は変わらず ～供給サイドが成長をけん引する一方、個人消費と不動産は_f9a4f0d8.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 19 日

1/6 
中国・７-９月 GDP は前年比＋4.5％に加速も、外需依存の構図は変わらず 
～供給サイドが成長をけん引する一方、個人消費と不動産は力強さを欠く展開が続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 中国経済は、世界的なＡＩ・半導体需要を背景に輸出や製造業が堅調に推移する一方、不動
産不況や雇用回復の遅れを背景に個人消費や投資は力強さを欠いており、 「強さ」 と 「弱さ」
が混在している。７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前年比＋4.5％と前期から加速し、堅調な外
需を追い風に、製造業、鉱業が景気を下支えする動きが確認された。ただし、企業部門では
商品市況上昇によるコスト増が進む一方、家計の節約志向から最終価格への転嫁は難しく、
企業部門のインフレと消費者部門のディスインフレが併存している。 
○ 政府は個人消費喚起策や財政 ・ 金融支援を強化しており、 ９月の小売売上高（前年比＋1.1％）
には持ち直しの兆しがみられた。ただ、その中身をみると、低価格業態やＥＣへの需要シフ
トが進む一方、 自動車や宝飾品、 娯楽関連などの需要は弱く、 家計の節約志向は依然根強い。
不動産市場も大都市の一部で改善の兆しがみられるものの、地方都市では住宅価格の下落が
続き、不動産投資（年初来前年比▲20.6％）は歴史的な低水準にある。一方、設備投資や公
共投資も含む固定資産投資（年初来前年比▲7.2％）には底打ちの兆しもみられる。 
○ 供給面では、 ９月の鉱工業生産が前年比＋5.5％と加速し、外需の旺盛な動きを追い風にした
ハイテク製造業を中心に堅調さが続いている。内需が弱いなか、中国経済は引き続き外需と
供給サイドへの依存度が高く、国内の需給不均衡も続いている。加えて、原材料価格の上昇
を製品価格へ十分転嫁できず企業収益が圧迫されていることや、不動産規制への懸念から中
国本土株は上値の重い展開が続いている。先行きについては、政策効果の及び方によって業
種間の業績格差が一段と広がる可能性には引き続き注意が必要と考えられる。

【外需の堅調さをけん引役に、７-９月ＧＤＰは前年比＋4.5％に伸びが加速している】 
足元の中国経済を巡っては、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・ 半導体関連需要の旺盛さを追い風に輸出
は堅調な動きをみせている。こうした動きを背景に、ＡＩ関連やＥＶ（電気自動車）など個別企業
は世界的な競争力を高めるなど、ミクロ面での強さがうかがえる。一方で、長引く不動産不況や若
年層を中心とする雇用回復の遅れなどが個人消費をはじめとする内需は力強さを欠いている。 この
ように、マクロ面でみた中国経済は弱さがうかがえるなど、相反する材料が混在している。

### Page 2

2/6 
こうした状況は、 足元の物価動向にも現れている。 イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油をはじめとす
る商品市況の上昇を反映して、川上段階の企業部門はインフレに直面している。さらに、企業間取
引段階においては価格転嫁の動きが広がりをみせる流れも確認できる。 しかし、 家計部門が節約志
向を強めるなかで、 企業部門にとっては最終製品への価格転嫁のハードルが高い状況が続いている。
その結果、消費者段階の物価は低迷するディスインフレ基調が続いている。このように、足元の中
国経済はインフレとディスインフレが共存する展開となっている（注１）。 
このように、足元の中国経済は「強さ」と「弱さ」が混在する状況にあるなか、７-９月の実質Ｇ
ＤＰ成長率は前年同期比＋4.5％となり、 前期 （同＋4.3％） から加速した （図１）。 前期比の成長率
も＋1.2％と前期（同＋0.9％）から加速
しており、年率換算では＋ 4.9％と試算
されるなど持ち直しの動きが確認され
ている。分野ごとの動きをみると、個人
消費など内需の弱さを反映して第３次
産業（サービス業）は頭打ちする一方、
第１次産業（農林漁業）の生産は堅調に
推移しているほか、 第２次産業 （製造業 ・
鉱業） の生産が持ち直したことが足元の
景気を下支えしている。イラン情勢の悪
化による商品市況の上昇を受けて、 鉱業
部門の生産が活発化しているほか、 輸出の堅調さが製造業の生産を押し上げる展開が続く。 前述の
ように川上段階でのインフレを受けて、 名目成長率 （前年同期比＋6.0％） は２四半期連続で実質成
長率を上回る伸びとなるなど、マクロ的には「デフレ脱却」が進んでいる。

【個人消費に一見持ち直しの兆しも、その内容をみれば依然として厳しい状況が続いている】 
個人消費をはじめとする内需が力強さを欠くなか、国務院は７月、 同国で初めて個人消費に焦点
を当てた 「消費拡大の５ヵ年計画」を公表した。 同計画では、2030 年の小売売上高 （社会消費品小
売総額）の規模を2025 年（50.1 兆元）に比べて約２割（60 兆元前後）引き上げ、その実現へ消費
構造の高度化や家計所得の引き上げを目指すとした。 加えて、 共産党は７月末に開催した中央政治
局会議において、 インフラ関連など財政支出の進捗加速による景気下支えを図る方針を示した。 財
政部・中銀（中国人民銀行） ・国家金融監督管理総局は、８月17 日付で内需促進を目的とする新た
な財政・金融支援策を発表し、中小・零細民間企業や消費者向け融資への利子補給を拡充すること
を明らかにしている。 
こうした一連の個人消費喚起に向けた動きも追い風に、 ９月の小売売上高 （社会消費品小売総額）

注１ 10 月14 日付レポート「足元の中国経済は、物価の二極化と外需依存が一段と鮮明に」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666553.html） 
図 1 実質 GDP(季節調整値)と成長率(前年比)の推移

### Page 3

3/6 
は前年同月比＋1.1％となり、前月（同
＋0.4）から加速して６ヵ月ぶりの高い
伸びとなった。 前月比も＋0.35％と前月
（同▲0.16％） から２ヵ月ぶりの拡大に
転じるとともに、 そのペースも３ヵ月ぶ
りの高い伸びとなるなど、 持ち直しの動
きが確認されている（図２） 。地域別で
は、都市部（前年比＋0.8％）の伸びが
加速しているものの、 農村 （同＋2.7％）
に比べて低い伸びにとどまるなど、 依然
として力強さを欠いている。近年のイン
ターネットの爆発的な普及を追い風に、 小売売上全体に占めるインターネットを通じたＥＣ （電子
商取引）の割合は拡大している。９月は大手ＥＣサイトの大型セールはないものの、中秋節商戦や
秋の新商品投入などを追い風に、オンライン販売は年初来前年比＋4.4％と前月（同＋4.3％）から
わずかに伸びが加速しており、小売売上全体を下支えした。店舗別では、コンビニエンスストアや
スーパーは前年を上回る伸びをみせる一方、 専門店や百貨店などは軒並み前年を下回る伸びにとど
まり、家計部門の節約志向の強まりを受けて、より低価格な業態への需要シフトが進んでいる。 
財別では、不動産に対する需要低迷が続くなかで建材（前年比▲9.2％）や家具（同▲3.0％）の
需要は引き続き低迷している。 耐久消費財の需要が弱いことに加え、 当局が実施した需要喚起策の
効果が一巡していることも重なり、自動車（前年比 ▲21.2％）は引き続き大幅に下振れする一方、
新型スマートフォンの発売などを反映して家電（前年比＋1.9％）は前年を上回る伸びで推移して
いる。しかし、雇用不安などを理由とする家計部門の節約志向が根強く残るなかで宝飾品（前年比
▲9.6％）のほか、娯楽関連（同▲1.1％）の需要も前年を下回っている。飲食料品など生活必需品
や、 日用品に対する需要には底堅い動きがみられるものの、 これはＥＣを通じた購買が常態化して
いることが影響しているとみられる。 家計部門の節約志向が一段と強まるなかでオンライン取引に
おける価格競争は激化しており、 結果的にディスインフレ圧力を脱することができない一因となっ
ている可能性がある。 このように、 個人消費を巡る動きは一見持ち直しの様相をみせているものの、
その内実については引き続き厳しい状況にあると捉えられる。

### Page 4

4/6 
【不動産投資は依然底がみえない一方、設備投資や公共投資には底打ちの兆しがでている】 
不動産不況が家計部門の節約志向に加え、不動産需要の足かせとなるなか、地方政府レベルでは
需要喚起に向けた様々な対策が打ち出されている。 こうした動きを追い風に、 一部の大都市では持
ち直しの兆しがうかがえる一方、 多くの地方都市では下落の動きに歯止めがかからない状況が続い
ている。 ９月の新築住宅価格指数も前年
同月比▲2.8％と前月（同▲3.0％）から
マイナス幅は縮小したものの、 前月比は
▲0.1％と 下 落に 歯止め が かか ら ない
（図３） 。一部の１級都市では中古住宅
もわずかに上昇しており、 最悪期を脱し
つつある様子がうかがえる。一方で、２
級都市や３級都市など地方都市では、 新
築・中古問わず住宅価格の下落が続いて
おり、地域ごとに「まだら模様」状態に
ある。なお、大都市部の新築住宅価格が
上昇に転じた背景には、 不動産開発会社の資金調達環境の厳しさを理由に供給が絞られていること
が影響しており、需要拡大の動きが価格上昇を促している状況にはほど遠い。 
９月の不動産投資も年初来前年比▲20.6％と前月 （同▲19.9％） からマイナス幅が拡大しており、
過去に遡って最もマイナス幅が大きくなっている。 当研究所が試算した単月ベースの前年同月比の
伸びは▲26.0％と前月（同▲22.5％）からマイナス幅は拡大しており、引き続き歴史的低水準で推
移している。分野別でも、住宅、オフィス、商業用不動産のいずれも低迷しているほか、建設から
販売にかけてすべての段階で大幅なマイナスとなっている。 地域別では、 東北地区で大幅減少が続
いているほか、 それ以外の地域も低迷しており、 地方都市による支援拡充の動きにもかかわらず資
金流入も細っており、不動産市場は底がみえない状況にある。 
企業部門による設備投資や公共投資 などを含む９月の固定資産投資も年初来前年比 ▲7.2％と６
ヵ月連続のマイナスで推移しており、マ
イナス幅は前月 （同▲7.2％） から横ばい
で推移している。当研究所が試算した単
月ベースの前年同月比の伸びは ▲7.2％
と前月（同▲10.4％）からマイナス幅は
縮小しており、前月比も＋0.07％と前月
（同▲0.55％）から７ヵ月ぶりの拡大に
転じるなど持ち直しの兆しがみられる
（図４） 。前述の通り、 当局が公共投資の
進捗促進を図る動きをみせていること
が影響している可能性がある。 とはい

5/6 
え、 固定資産投資の規模は依然としてコロナ禍の影響が最も色濃く現れた時期をも下回っていると
試算されるなど、足元の状況は極めて厳しいことには変わりがない。

【中国景気は引き続き供給サイドがけん引役となる状況は変わらない】 
供給動向を示す９月の鉱工業生産は前年同月比＋5.5％となり、前月（同＋5.2％）から加速して
６ヵ月ぶりの高い伸びとなった。前月比も＋0.61％と前月（同＋0.54％）から拡大ペースが加速し
ており、持ち直しの動きを強めている様
子がうかがえるなど、足元においても供
給サイドが景気のけん引役となってい
る状況は変わらない（図５） 。前述の通
り、内需は消費、投資ともに持ち直しの
兆しがみられるものの、依然として力強
さを欠く推移をみせていることを勘案
すれば、中国国内では需給を巡る不均衡
状態が長期化している。したがって、中
国国内における余剰生産を解消する観
点でも、足元の景気は外需に依存せざる
を得ない状況が続いていると捉えられる。 
分野別では、 製造業 （前年比＋6.0％） の生産は引き続き堅調に推移している。 なかでも外需の堅
調さをけん引しているハイテク製造業（前年比＋15.3％）は高い伸びで推移しており、外需の堅調
さが生産活動を押し上げる展開が続いている。 また、 異常気象の頻発を受けてエネルギー需要が押
し上げられていることを反映して、 エネルギー関連 （前年比＋5.6％） も底堅い動きをみせている。
さらに、 原油をはじめとする商品市況が高止まりしていることを受けて、 国内供給を拡大させるべ
く鉱業 （前年比＋1.3％） も前年を上回る伸びに転じており、 資源調達の多様化を目指す動きが中国
国内の生産動向にも影響していると考えられる。 
財別では、 習近平指導部が主導する新質生産力のほか、 サプライチェーンの自立自強への取り組
みを反映して集積回路（前年比＋18.4％）や産業用ロボット（同＋18.9％）はともに高い伸びとな
るなど、旺盛な動きが続いている。一方で、需要喚起策の効果一巡を反映して、自動車（前年比
▲5.7％）のほか、マイコン（同▲17.0％） 、携帯電話（同▲26.9％）は軒並み減少するとともに、
マイナス幅も拡大している。 不動産需要の低迷が続くとともに、 供給も抑制されていることを反映
して、粗鋼（前年比＋0.1％）や銑鉄（同▲1.4％） 、鋼材（同▲3.8％）の生産は引き続き低迷して
おり、 板ガラス （同▲14.0％） やセメント （同▲5.3％） もマイナスとなるなど、 中国国内での過剰
生産能力の縮小に向けた取り組みも生産の足かせとなっている。 イラン情勢の不透明感を受けた原
油高が長期化するなか、 当局が石油製品の国内供給を優先して在庫が積み上がる一方、 精製マージ
ンは低迷しており、 石油精製量 （前年比▲8.4％） は低迷している。 さらに、 原油高を受けて世界的
に再生可能エネルギーの需要が高まっているものの、太陽光電池（前年比 ▲5.1％）や発電機（同
